### 1. user (2026-07-02T06:11:32.991Z)

Four pre-existing tests fail on origin/master (unrelated to any current work) because PR #177 added a WSL readability preflight (`wsl_source_unreadable_reason`) to `WasbRunner._stage_video` in `backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py`. That preflight issues an extra `_wsl_bash` call running `test -r ... && echo READABLE` BEFORE the `cp`. These tests mock `_wsl_bash` to return a single CompletedProcess without "READABLE" in stdout, so the preflight now reports the source unreadable and `_stage_video` returns None, failing the test.

The sibling file `tests/test_tracknet_runner.py` was already updated for this (see `test_stage_video_success` / `test_stage_video_failure` there: they use `side_effect=[_proc(0, stdout="READABLE"), _proc(0, stdout="OK")]`). The following WASB tests were NOT updated and need the same treatment (make the mock return "READABLE" for the preflight call, or patch `wsl_source_unreadable_reason`/`_stage_video` directly):

1. `tests/test_wasb_runner.py::test_stage_video_success_and_failure`
2. `tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py::test_wasb_stage_video_cmd_has_no_quoted_tilde`
3. `tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py::test_wasb_run_predict_resolves_relative_out_dir`
4. `tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py::test_wasb_run_predict_inference_cmd_has_no_quoted_tilde`

Reproduce: `python -m pytest tests/test_wasb_runner.py tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py -q` (4 failures, all with the "video source is not readable from WSL" ERROR log). Fix the mocks so each test's `fake_wsl`/`_wsl_bash` returns stdout containing "READABLE" for the `test -r` preflight call (the call whose command contains `test -r`), then the cp/inference calls as before. Verify the whole suite is green afterwards and run `ruff check` on the changed test files. Branch from origin/master explicitly, one focused PR, do not merge.

1	"""WSL tilde-quoting regression tests.
2	
3	shlex.quote('~/clips/x.mp4') single-quotes the whole path, so bash never
4	expands ~ and staging fails with "cannot create regular file '~/clips/...'".
5	This broke the live trajectory-hybrid path for the DEFAULT ~/clips stage dir
6	(found by the 2026-06-11 wasb_hybrid smoke run; masked until then because
7	trajectories were generated via manual WSL scripts and tests mock _wsl_bash).
8	"""
9	from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
10	
11	import pytest
12	
13	from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import wsl_tilde_quote
14	from backend.pipeline.detectors.wasb_runner import WasbRunner, WasbConfig
15	from backend.pipeline.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner, TrackNetConfig
16	
17	
18	@pytest.mark.parametrize("path,expected", [
19	    ("~/clips/x.mp4", "~/clips/x.mp4"),            # safe chars: quote is a no-op, ~ expandable
20	    ("~/clips/has space.mp4", "~/'clips/has space.mp4'"),
21	    ("~", "~"),                                     # bare home
22	    ("/mnt/c/Users/x/out", "/mnt/c/Users/x/out"),   # shlex.quote no-op for safe paths
23	    ("/mnt/c/has space", "'/mnt/c/has space'"),     # ordinary quoting preserved
24	])
25	def test_wsl_tilde_quote(path, expected):
26	    assert wsl_tilde_quote(path) == expected
27	
28	
29	def _proc(rc=0, stdout="OK", stderr=""):
30	    p = MagicMock()
31	    p.returncode = rc
32	    p.stdout = stdout
33	    p.stderr = stderr
34	    return p
35	
36	
37	def _no_quoted_tilde(cmd: str) -> bool:
38	    return "'~" not in cmd and '"~' not in cmd
39	
40	
41	def test_wasb_stage_video_cmd_has_no_quoted_tilde():
42	    r = WasbRunner(WasbConfig())  # defaults: wsl_stage_dir="~/clips"
43	    captured = []
44	
45	    def fake_wsl(cmd):
46	        captured.append(cmd)
47	        return _proc(stdout="READABLE") if "test -r" in cmd else _proc()
48	
49	    with patch.object(r, "_wsl_bash", side_effect=fake_wsl):
50	        dest = r._stage_video("C:\\vids\\clip.mp4", "clip__abc.mp4")
51	    assert dest == "~/clips/clip__abc.mp4"
52	    # captured[0] = readability preflight (test -r), captured[1] = the staging cp.
53	    stage_cmd = next(c for c in captured if c.startswith("mkdir -p"))
54	    assert _no_quoted_tilde(stage_cmd), stage_cmd
55	    assert "mkdir -p ~/clips" in stage_cmd
56	
57	
58	def test_tracknet_stage_video_cmd_has_no_quoted_tilde():
59	    r = TrackNetRunner(TrackNetConfig())
60	    captured = []
61	
62	    def fake_wsl(cmd):
63	        captured.append(cmd)
64	        return _proc(stdout="READABLE") if "test -r" in cmd else _proc()
65	
66	    with patch.object(r, "_wsl_bash", side_effect=fake_wsl):
67	        r._stage_video("C:\\vids\\clip.mp4", "clip__abc.mp4")
68	    stage_cmd = next(c for c in captured if c.startswith("mkdir -p"))
69	    assert _no_quoted_tilde(stage_cmd), stage_cmd
70	
71	def test_wasb_run_predict_resolves_relative_out_dir(tmp_path, monkeypatch):

### 54. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.994Z)

1	# Golden / GPU-Extractor Data Sharing — GPU box ⇄ dev sessions
2	
3	**Status:** convention + plan (2026-06-15). Tracked in **#175**.
4	**Purpose:** a single cloud-synced location for the GPU-extracted golden artifacts (trajectory CSVs, `*_golden_features.json`, manifests, baseline snapshots) so the **GPU box** and **CPU dev/agent sessions** share the same files — without committing large data to git.
5	
6	## Why this exists
7	The rally-quality measurement is split across two machines by capability:
8	- **`backend/eval/fusion_golden.py`** (shuttle+person feature **extraction**) needs the **videos**, the **trajectory CSVs**, the **`.rallies.csv` golden labels**, and a **GPU/WSL** stack → only the GPU box can run it.
9	- **`backend/eval/fusion_compare.py`** / **`backend/eval/ablation.py`** (the LOVO **litmus**: ΔF1, bootstrap CI, paired sign test) are **pure-CPU re-scoring** that read only the small `*_golden_features.json` → any session (incl. a CPU dev container) can run them.
10	
11	Today those artifacts sit in gitignored `output/` (per-machine) and scattered local folders (`...\Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories\`, `...\Golden Labelled\`). Nothing syncs them between machines, so a CPU session cannot see the GPU box's extraction. This wires that gap with OneDrive.
12	
13	## Canonical shared folder
14	`%USERPROFILE%\OneDrive\rally-annotator\golden-data\`  →  `C:\Users\avidu\OneDrive\rally-annotator\golden-data\`
15	
16	OneDrive is present + signed-in on both the GPU box and the dev machine under the same account, so it auto-syncs. Layout (date/size-tagged so a re-extraction is a new folder, never an overwrite):
17	
18	```
19	golden-data/
20	  README.md
21	  features/<corpus-tag>/       *_golden_features.json     (small — the key shared artifact)
22	  trajectories/<corpus-tag>/   *_predict.csv / *_wasb.csv (medium — reusable across runs)
23	  manifests/                   *_lovo_manifest.json
24	  baselines/                   leaderboard / regression snapshots (or keep tracked in eval_baselines/)
25	```
26	
27	`<corpus-tag>` = e.g. `2026-06-15-n6` (date + corpus size). **Do NOT** put raw videos here (tens of GB) — keep those local to the GPU box.
28	
29	## The GPU ⇄ CPU workflow
30	1. **GPU box** (videos + CSVs + labels present):
31	   ```
32	   python -m backend.eval.fusion_golden --manifest <manifest> \
33	     --out-dir "%USERPROFILE%\OneDrive\rally-annotator\golden-data\features\2026-06-15-n6"
34	   ```
35	   (or extract to local `output/` then copy the `*_golden_features.json` into the shared folder).
36	2. OneDrive syncs the JSONs to the dev machine automatically (small files, seconds).
37	3. **CPU dev/agent session** points the analysis at the shared folder:
38	   ```
39	   python -m backend.eval.fusion_compare --features-dir "...\golden-data\features\2026-06-15-n6" --record
40	   python -m backend.eval.ablation       --features-dir "...\golden-data\features\2026-06-15-n6" --granularity group
41	   ```

### 62. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.994Z)

This is a genuinely deep problem and you're right to push on it — let me both **educate** and **research/design** it properly. First, the key conceptual correction, then I'm launching a dedicated deep-research + design effort.

## The pushback you invited (the part I'm confident about now)

**1. "Secret file so users can run tests without knowing how it's tested" — this is partially impossible, and recognizing why points to the *right* solution.** If a contributor on another machine must *run* the suite, the test process must read the fixture data in plaintext at some moment — which means the data **and** any decryption key are both on their machine. Anyone who can run the test can, with effort, dump what it read. This is the DRM/"analog hole" problem: you cannot hand someone a locked box *and* the key and expect the box to stay locked. Encryption only hides data from parties **without** the key — i.e., the public browsing a repo, a leaked clone, or a CI log — **not** from the trusted contributor running the tests.

**2. But that's fine — because your real goal isn't secrecy, it's *non-attributability*.** You said it yourself: "we do not want someone to attribute the structured data to the exact video... by itself useless to any public user." That is a *much* more achievable and well-defined property than secrecy, and it's the correct framing:
   - **Unlinkability** — strip everything that ties the numbers to a specific match/video/person (notably: *don't* key fixtures by `video_id = MD5(file)` — that's a fingerprint of the source file; use opaque salted IDs; drop titles, timestamps, URLs, player names).
   - **Minimization** — commit only the numeric streams the test needs (trajectory + signal columns + GT intervals + expected output); never frames, thumbnails, audio, or person crops.
   - **Uselessness-in-isolation** — anonymous `(frame, x, y)` numbers reveal neither your corpus nor your method.

**3. Protobuf ≠ secrecy.** Protobuf is a *great* choice for compactness, schema-versioning, and deterministic serialization — but a binary blob is trivially decoded. It buys you schema discipline, not protection. Protection (against the no-key public) is a *separate* layer: `git-crypt` / SOPS+`age` / out-of-repo fetch. So the architecture is naturally **tiered**: a public, anonymized, unencrypted tier for the logic/refactor tests everyone can run, and an optional encrypted/key-gated tier for richer or more sensitive data.

**4. The decisive question is provenance.** Whether derived data even carries the source's constraints splits hard on: *did you record the corpus yourself (your own IP — concern is trade-secret/privacy)* vs *is it third-party broadcast/YouTube (copyright + ToS)*. And if the source is a **public** video, an adversary could re-derive trajectories from it and correlate — so non-attributability is far stronger for a **private, owner-recorded** corpus. This is the crux the research must resolve.

I'm launching a deep legal/IP/privacy research + crypto-design workflow now (covers IN/US/EU, copyright-of-facts, EU database right, ToS, GDPR/DPDP, biometrics, re-identification, plus the tiered protobuf/encryption architecture), and asking three gating questions in parallel so the final synthesis is grounded in your actual situation rather than my guesses.

### 77. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.995Z)

I now have the full first workflow (the fixture *mechanics*), and your three answers are locked in. The second workflow — the legal memo (IN/US/EU) plus the non-attributable/encryption architecture and red-team — is still running; I'll deliver the **single unified written plan** when it lands. Here's the high-signal interim, because two findings already in hand are genuinely important and shape everything.

## Your answers → a clean convergence

- **Owned corpus** → this is the *easy* legal fork: no third-party copyright/ToS infringement; it's your IP. The concern collapses to trade-secret/competitive-moat (your call to publish) + player privacy. And non-attributability is **strongest** here — a private corpus has *nothing public to correlate against*, so the fingerprinting/re-identification attack (re-run the detector on a public video, match the trajectory) simply has no reference and fails.
- **Private now, may open-source later** → git history is permanent, so we design the public tier to be **public-grade from the first commit** (no scrubbing later).
- **Adults, consent not formalized** → person/pose-derived streams stay **out of the public tier** (derived-personal-data risk under DPDP/GDPR without consent); the public tier is **shuttle-only**.

**The elegant part:** the privacy-driven choice (publish shuttle-only, keep person streams private) lands on the *exact same* minimal tier that the determinism analysis independently demands — see below. Privacy and correctness point at one design. That's the spine of the "Byzantine" solution.

## The killer finding from the mechanics workflow

The design's natural seam is right — **freeze the post-detector trajectory CSV** (`Frame,Visibility,X,Y`) and replay the deterministic pipeline from there, reusing the *entire shipped eval stack* (`metrics.py`, `experiment.py`, `windowing.py`, `regression.py`, `eval_baselines/`), with two gates: a **characterization/snapshot** gate (catches any refactor side-effect) and a **quality-floor** gate (catches quality drops). Synthetic fixtures ship first (licensing-clean by construction); owned real fixtures are owner-gated.

But the adversarial critic caught a **fatal determinism flaw**, code-verified: the *learned* variants (`shuttle_only`, `fusion`) run 1000 epochs of numpy gradient descent on OpenBLAS compiled `DYNAMIC_ARCH` — the float-summation order is chosen per CPU microarchitecture, the resulting probabilities sit right on the 0.5 keep/drop cliff, so an external contributor on a **different CPU gets different integer tp/fp/fn on byte-identical correct code**. The gate goes red, they're told to "refresh the snapshot," they commit *their* machine's numbers, and snapshot diffs become noise reviewers learn to ignore — destroying the one property you're building this for.

**Fix (and the convergence):** the cross-machine gate must be **STATIC-ONLY** — the pure-stdlib `CONTROL` path (gate → merge → score) plus pure shuttle features — with learned/person scoring kept **owner-box-only**. That's *exactly* the shuttle-only, person-excluded tier privacy already requires. Two other code-verified must-fixes: route freeze **and** both gates through one shared `replay_fixture()` helper (or the snapshot doesn't actually exercise the windowing "brain" it claims to lock), and **parse-compare JSON, never byte-compare** (CRLF/float-format would false-RED on Windows — your `core.autocrlf=true` is set).

## What's still cooking

The legal memo (copyright-of-facts, EU database right, India DPDP, ToS, biometrics, re-ID) and the tiered protobuf/encryption + key-management architecture + red-team are mid-flight. When they complete I'll merge all three into one plan and **write it to `docs/GOLDEN_REGRESSION_FIXTURES.md`** for your review (I won't commit or open a PR — per your conventions). I'll flag this to you the moment it's ready.

711	    "memo": {
712	      "executiveSummary": "INTERNAL MEMO — general legal information, NOT legal advice; retained India + EU/UK counsel sign-off required before any public commit. QUESTION: can the project commit DERIVED numeric data (per-frame shuttle (x,y) coordinates, rally start/end time-intervals, audio/person/court feature columns, ground-truth intervals) into a public GitHub repo so external contributors can run the rally-segmentation regression suite WITHOUT the videos? BOTTOM LINE: Yes, but only under conditions, and the analysis FORKS on data provenance. The OUTPUT data itself is, across all three jurisdictions, essentially uncopyrightable FACT/measurement — it does not inherit the source video's copyright (US: Feist + 17 USC 102(b) + NBA v. Motorola; India: EBC v. D.B. Modak, no sui generis right; EU: Football Dataco v. Yahoo — copyright protects only original selection/arrangement, never the numbers). So a thin compilation copyright at most attaches to a creative schema, never to the coordinates. BUT copyright is the easy axis and NOT where the risk lives. The real exposure is fourfold and provenance- and stream-dependent: (1) the ACT of ingesting/extracting from the source video needs its own lawful basis — owned/permissioned video is clean; a US fair-use defense for analytic extraction is now CONTESTED, not \"strongly favorable,\" after Thomson Reuters v. Ross (2025) and the US Copyright Office May-2025 report, and unlawful acquisition independently destroys the defense (Bartz v. Anthropic, ~$1.5B settlement); (2) CONTRACT / Terms-of-Service can bar extraction and redistribution even where copyright cannot reach the facts (ProCD; hiQ v. LinkedIn; YouTube ToS bars scraping AND \"harvest information that might identify a person (for example... faces)\" AND redistribution) — and the remedy is injunction + forced deletion of the committed dataset, not nominal damages; (3) EU/UK sui generis DATABASE RIGHT may subsist in self-derived match-event/coordinate streams because, on the leading Football Dataco v. Sportradar reasoning, recording pre-existing on-court facts you do not control is \"OBTAINING,\" not \"creating\" — so the project's comfortable \"we created it\" premise is genuinely contestable; (4) PRIVACY — person/pose/gait streams and anything involving MINORS are the highest-risk and several should NOT be published at all. The cleanest defensible path: commit ONLY owned-or-permissioned-source, minors-excluded, person/pose/gait-EXCLUDED (shuttle + abstract rally-interval + non-biometric feature) data, stripped of every source-attribution field (no filename, no video_id MD5, no match metadata), under a no-extraction-from-third-party-source and no-redistribution hygiene posture, with optional encryption only as a barrier against the no-key public (it cannot bind a contributor who runs the tests).",
713	      "derivedDataCoreVerdict": "The derived numeric data DOES NOT inherit the source video's copyright in any of the three jurisdictions — this is the one firmly-settled conclusion. Per-frame shuttle (x,y) coordinates, rally start/end timestamps, and audio/person/court feature values are uncopyrightable FACTS/measurements about a recorded physical event: discovered, not authored. US: Feist (facts never copyrightable; \"originality, not sweat of the brow, is the touchstone\"), 17 USC 102(b) (no copyright in any \"discovery\"), NBA v. Motorola (facts generated by a sporting event are uncopyrightable \"purely factual information which any patron... could acquire from the arena\" even though the broadcast is protected). A coordinate/timestamp table is also NOT a 17 USC 101 \"derivative work\": a derivative work recasts the original's protected EXPRESSION (framing, editing, imagery, commentary), and a number table carries none of it. India: EBC v. D.B. Modak — copyright needs \"skill and judgment,\" protects only original arrangement, not facts; no EU-style sui generis right exists. EU: Football Dataco v. Yahoo ...

The split is by SENSITIVITY, not by file format.\n\nTIER-0 — PUBLIC, UNENCRYPTED, NO KEY. A small, de-attributed, Fork-A-only, BIOMETRIC-FREE fixture set committed under a NEW tracked dir `eval_baselines/fixtures/` (extending the existing committed-baseline precedent — regression.py:33 already comments that eval_baselines/ is \"NOT under the gitignored output/, so a fresh checkout/CI can load a committed baseline\"). ANY cloner with no key runs the SHUTTLE-ONLY regression path (experiment.predict/compare LOVO ΔF1; ablation.run_ablation; the Gate-A litmus shape; regression-style floor vs GT). Contents are minimized to: opaque `fixture_id`, `fps`/`frame_width` scale constants, per-candidate `start`/`end` (time-origin reset), the 5 SHUTTLE columns (duration, peak_velocity, mean_velocity, active_ratio, net_crossings — inanimate-object, fps/res-invariant), integer `label`, and relative `gts`. The PERSON block and AUDIO block are EXCLUDED from Tier-0 (person = the legal memo's Art.9 \"tripwire\" / DPDP singling-out risk in a small roster). Tier-0 tests are UNCONDITIONAL — they run on every PR, converting the currently-skipped litmus into a real public gate.\n\nTIER-1 — RICHER / SENSITIVE, KEY-GATED, SKIPS CLEANLY WHEN ABSENT. (B) ENCRYPTED-IN-REPO (SOPS+age) only for Fork-A-clean-but-still-private data where in-repo versioning is genuinely wanted; decrypt to a gitignored path before the Tier-1 run. A Tier-1 test SKIPS (never fails) when the key/data is absent, reusing the proven `_golden_present()` skipif pattern (tests/test_ablation.py:366-371) generalized to a shared `_tier1_present()` helper. USELESSNESS-TO-THE-NO-KEY-PUBLIC comes from minimization (opaque numbers, no schema-external meaning) PLUS optional Tier-1 encryption. trajectory_to_action_windows(

    fps: float,
    frame_width: float,
    chunk_start: float = 0.0,

    merge_gap: float = 2.0,

That puts the `CLAUDE.md` workflow convention + `docs/PROJECT_DOC_TEMPLATE.md` on master — the *project-local, checked-in* enforcement that future projects follow the template. Plus the cross-session memory [[working-agreement-tracked-project-docs]]. CI was green for it (the billing outage is resolved).

**Executing M1 now.** A background coding agent is implementing the framework in the worktree:
- `backend/eval/golden_fixtures.py` — the shared `replay_fixture()` (CONTROL/pure-stdlib, **no numpy** → cross-machine stable) + a freeze/refresh CLI.
- 3 **synthetic** fixtures (generated by the freeze tool, not hand-authored) + manifest + `.gitattributes` LF pin.
- `tests/test_golden_fixtures.py` — parse-compared characterization (exact-int / approx-float), a positive "no person/audio keys" privacy assertion, a perturbation test proving windows are re-derived, and an idempotent-freeze test.

It bypasses the `fusion_compare`/`ablation` loaders (which `KeyError` on person-free fixtures — a verified red-team catch) by building `VideoCandidates` directly.

When it finishes I'll **verify myself** (full `run_all_tests.py` + `ruff` + `mypy`, per the pre-LGTM gate), review the diff, and **merge** M1 with CI green — then continue down the §7 tracker (M2 freeze-tool hardening → M3/M4 gates → P1 de-attribution tooling), holding P0 until the windowing churn settles and P2/P3 for counsel. I'll flag you as each lands.

## Summary

Implemented deliverable **M1** (Fixture framework + synthetic Tier-0 + shared replay helper) of the Golden Regression Fixtures project, entirely within the `golden-fixtures` worktree. No git commands beyond read-only `status` for verification.

### Files created
- `backend/eval/golden_fixtures.py` — the shared module: `CURRENT_SCHEMA=1`, `FIXTURES_DIR` (resolved from `__file__`, parents[2]), `make_synthetic_trajectory`, `replay_fixture`, `freeze_fixture`, `freeze_synthetic`, `refresh_snapshot`, `load_manifest`, `load_fixture`, `compare_expected`, and the `__main__` CLI (`--freeze-synthetic` / `--refresh-snapshot [--slug|--all]` / `--check`).
- `eval_baselines/fixtures/&lt;slug&gt;/{trajectory.csv, labels.csv, expected.json, provenance.json}` for the 3 slugs (generated by `--freeze-synthetic`, never hand-written).
- `eval_baselines/fixtures/manifest.json` — array of `{slug, fps, frame_width, windowing:{vt,mg,mwd,max_win}, kind:"synthetic", schema_version, paths}`.
- `eval_baselines/fixtures/README.md` — guardrails + freeze/refresh commands + "generated, never hand-edited" + §6 honest-limits.
- `.gitattributes` (worktree root) — `eval_baselines/fixtures/** text eol=lf`.
- `tests/test_golden_fixtures.py` — parametrized over `load_manifest()` slugs.

### Verify results (verbatim)
1. `--freeze-synthetic` → `froze 3 synthetic fixture(s): clean_single_rally, multi_rally_with_deadtime, occlusion_break`. `--check` → all `[ok]`, `EXIT=0`.
2. `python run_all_tests.py` → **744 passed, 7 skipped** (the pre-existing skips; new file adds 17 tests, all green). With CI coverage flags: **Total coverage 80.36%** ("Required test coverage of 70% reached"); the new module itself is at 85%.
3. `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!` (EXIT 0). `mypy backend training` → `Success: no issues found in 103 source files` (EXIT 0). One initial ruff finding (unused `field` import) fixed.

### The 3 synthetic scenarios (num candidates, num segments, F1)
| slug | candidates | segments | F1 | what it exercises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `clean_single_rally` | 1 | 1 | 1.0 | one ~4s in-flight rally → single window, net_crossings=7 |
| `multi_rally_with_deadtime` | 2 | 2 | 1.0 | two rallies split by 3s of **still** (low-velocity) dead-time |
| `occlusion_break` | 2 | 2 | 1.0 | two rallies split by 3s of **occlusion** (Visibility=0) |

### Hard-constraint compliance
- **CONTROL/pure-stdlib only**: builds `VideoCandidates` directly with only the 5 shuttle columns and replays via `experiment.predict(CONTROL, vc)`; never calls `fusion_compare.load_videos`/`ablation.load_videos` (verified `CONTROL.is_learned() is False`).
- **Parse-compare, never byte-compare**: `compare_expected` does `json.load` on both sides — exact on int/structural fields (candidate count, `net_crossings` as int, TP/FP/FN, segment count, slug, schema, gt_hash) and abs-tol 1e-6 on floats.
- **Windowing pinned per fixture**: `(vt=0.02, mg=2.0, mwd=2.0, max_win=0.0)` written into each `provenance.json` and the manifest, read back and passed explicitly to `build_candidates` — does not depend on the named `SERVED` constant.
- **Determinism**: trajectories from deterministic sine formulae (no `random`); all floats rounded to 6dp; UTF-8 / LF-only / trailing-newline confirmed.
- **Privacy**: every fixture carries `kind:"synthetic"`, `minors_free:true`, `license:"synthetic"`; `expected.json` has no person/pose/gait/face/audio/player fields (positive assertion test scans the raw text and the parsed candidate keys).

Beyond the spec's required tests I added `test_check_cli_passes`, `test_synthetic_provenance_tags`, `test_control_is_not_learned`, and `test_manifest_is_non_empty`, and I additionally demonstrated out-of-band that `--check` returns exit 1 on a seeded trajectory perturbation (then restored) — proving the gate catches behaviour changes, not just echoes the snapshot.

### Deviations from spec
- **One** minor wording in `test_perturbing_trajectory_changes_segments`: I perturb by **truncating** the trajectory to its first half (which shrinks the derived window's `end`) rather than deleting an interior hole. Reason: deleting interior rows does not move the segment boundaries — `merge_gap=2.0s` coalesces across the gap and the surviving rows keep their original frame indices, so start/end are unchanged. Truncation guarantees a structural change and still proves the windows are re-derived from the CSV (not echoed). No API or fixture-content deviation otherwise.

Note: numpy and cv2 *are* installed in this environment, so the full suite ran here rather than skipping — but the M1 replay path itself touches no numpy (CONTROL/stdlib), so it remains valid on a clean no-GPU clone.</result>

=== ci.yml ===
name: CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [master]
  pull_request:

permissions:
  contents: read

concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  # Lint gate (ruff.toml at repo root): pyflakes/pycodestyle + bugbear.
  # Also the repo-wide syntax guard — ruff reports invalid-syntax on every
  # tracked .py, including scripts/ and training/, which the backend-only
  # py_compile smoke sweep does not reach.
  lint:
    name: Ruff lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
        with:
          python-version: "3.13"
      - name: Install ruff
        run: python -m pip install ruff
      - name: Ruff check
        run: ruff check . --output-format github

  # Type gate (mypy.ini at repo root): lenient green baseline with an explicit
  # per-module quarantine list — ratchet by removing entries as modules clean
  # up. Typed core deps are installed so mypy sees their real signatures
  # (without them ignore_missing_imports would Any-stub half the checks).
  typecheck:
    name: Mypy (lenient baseline)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
        with:
          python-version: "3.13"
      - name: Install mypy + typed core deps
        run: python -m pip install mypy pydantic fastapi uvicorn python-dotenv google-genai numpy
      - name: Mypy
        run: mypy backend training

  # Fast guard against the 7fbbeb0 class of breakage: a SyntaxError or
  # module-level import error shipping while the suite stays green.
  # Deliberately does NOT install cv2/torch/numpy — the smoke tests must hold
  # on machines without the GPU stack (test_import_smoke stubs what's absent).
  import-smoke:
    name: Syntax sweep + import smoke (no GPU stack)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
        with:
          python-version: "3.13"
      - name: Install minimal deps
        run: python -m pip install pytest fastapi uvicorn pydantic python-dotenv google-genai
      - name: Run import smoke tests
        run: python -m pytest tests/test_import_smoke.py -v

  full-suite:
    name: Full test suite (offline, mocked)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
        with:
          python-version: "3.13"
          cache: pip
      - name: System libs for opencv-python
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update
          sudo apt-get install -y libgl1 libglib2.0-0t64 || sudo apt-get install -y libgl1 libglib2.0-0
      - name: Install CPU-only torch (no CUDA wheels on a CPU runner)
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## Summary

Hardened the Golden Regression Fixtures feature per the adversarial audit. All 17 confirmed fixes implemented. The 3 committed synthetic fixtures' `expected.json` (and CSVs/provenance) remain **byte-identical** — I hardened the freeze-real / edge paths + docs only.

### Files changed

- `tests/test_golden_fixtures.py` — fix #11 (by-kind provenance tags).
- `tests/test_golden_real_fixtures.py` — fix #2 (parametrized invariance) + fixes #1/#3/#4/#5/#6/#7/#10 negative tests.

- `docs/GOLDEN_REGRESSION_FIXTURES.md` — fixes #13–#17.
- `eval_baselines/fixtures/README.md` — fix #9 (numpy wording).

### Fix notes worth flagging
- **#1** label now validated against `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_\-]*$` (free-text like "mens singles final" → `RefusalError`); `source_note` is accepted-but-NOT-persisted (dropped from committed provenance, still scanned defensively).
- **#3** real-fixture freeze now writes to a temp dir and `os.replace`-promotes only after the privacy scan passes; negative reset labels are refused up-front — a refused/failed freeze leaves **no** partial dir.
- **#2 (important caveat):** Bit-identical reset-vs-no-reset scores are **not achievable for arbitrary sub-second offsets**, and I confirmed this is mathematically irreducible: the trajectory shifts by integer frames while labels live on the 6dp CSV grid, and 6dp-rounding is non-additive (`round6(8/30) − round6(7/30) ≠ round6(1/30)`). I implemented the prompt's prescribed `t0_sec`+label 6dp-rounding (eliminates double-rounding), which keeps the **genuine/raw** metric delta at the float-error floor (~3.3e-7, well within `_FLOAT_TOL`); it is exactly bit-identical for whole-second offsets. The only residual is a one-6dp-ULP (1e-6) difference in the *serialized* boundary error on sub-second offsets — the serialization grid, not a metric divergence. The test asserts raw-score invariance within `_FLOAT_TOL` (and exact snapshot equality for whole-second offsets), and I corrected the doc/docstring claims from "bit-identical" to "metric-invariant within `_FLOAT_TOL`; bit-identical for whole-second offsets" (honesty norm).

### Verification (verbatim)
1. `--check`: `3/3 fixtures match` — synthetic replay unchanged. Re-freeze confirms all synthetic `expected.json`/`provenance.json`/CSV bytes reproduce exactly.
2. Full suite: `784 passed, 7 skipped` — total coverage **80.71%** (≥ 80.4% floor; +0.22% vs prior 80.49%). `golden_fixtures.py` at 89%. The 7 warnings are pre-existing `ResourceWarning: unclosed database` from unrelated sqlite tests.
3. `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`; `mypy backend training` → `Success: no issues found in 103 source files`.
4. Fix #1 demonstrated into a tmp dir (not `eval_baselines/fixtures/`): `label="mens singles final"` rejected with no partial dir; a safe `source_note` left no `source_note` key in committed provenance (boilerplate `note` kept, `label` committed verbatim); a path-bearing `source_note` tripped the defensive scan.

No real fixtures were committed; `eval_baselines/fixtures/` contains only the 3 synthetic fixtures + manifest + README. No git commands were run.

278	### Q6: How can I quickly test the indexing without waiting to parse an entire multi-GB video file?
279	You can pass the `--max-frames <N>` argument in CLI or `max_frames` in the POST API payload. This restricts the segmenter frame loop to process only the first $N$ sub-sampled frames, enabling you to test motion filters and court relevance checks in seconds:
280	```bash
281	python -m backend.main --video-path "C:\Users\avidu\Videos\LargeTestVideo.MP4" --max-frames 300
282	```
283	*(At 5 sub-sampled frames per second, `--max-frames 300` analyzes exactly the first 60 seconds of video).*
[/external_agent_tool_result]

19	"""
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import argparse
23	import re

25	import sys
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple
28	
29	#: Code + committed-data surfaces scanned (NOT docs/scratch/output — those are intentional notes).
30	SCAN_ROOTS: Tuple[str, ...] = ("backend", "training", "tests", "scripts", "tools", "eval_baselines")
31	
32	#: This tool + its test legitimately contain sample patterns — never scan them.
33	SELF_EXCLUDE: Tuple[str, ...] = ("tools/leak_scan.py", "tests/test_leak_scan.py")
34	
35	#: A run of EXACTLY 32 hex chars (the MD5 video_id shape). The negative look-around means a 16-hex
36	#: ``fx_`` slug, a 12-hex ``gt_hash``, or a 40/64-hex SHA do NOT match.
37	_MD5_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9a-fA-F])[0-9a-fA-F]{32}(?![0-9a-fA-F])")
38	_WIN_USER_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z]:[\\/]+Users[\\/]+([^\\/\s\"'<>]+)")
39	_NIX_USER_RE = re.compile(r"/(?:home|Users)/([^/\s\"'<>]+)/")
40	
41	#: Tokens that are a DOCUMENTED placeholder, not a real username — so a doc'd path doesn't trip.
42	_PLACEHOLDERS = frozenset({
43	    "<name>", "<user>", "<username>", "<you>", "you", "youruser", "yourname", "user", "username",
44	})
45	
46	_ALLOW = "# leakscan-allow"
47	_TEXT_EXT = {".py", ".json", ".txt", ".csv", ".yml", ".yaml", ".cfg", ".ini", ".toml", ".md"}
48	
49	
50	def _tracked_files(root: Path) -> List[str]:
51	    """The tracked files under SCAN_ROOTS (via ``git ls-files`` so only committed paths are scanned)."""
52	    out = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", *SCAN_ROOTS], cwd=str(root),
53	                         capture_output=True, text=True)
54	    return [f.strip() for f in out.stdout.splitlines() if f.strip()]

237	    # --- (d) consistent time-origin reset (default ON; metric-invariant within _FLOAT_TOL, §4c) --
238	    # Subtract the SAME offset from BOTH the trajectory frames and the label seconds: metrics.score
239	    # is built from pure differences (interval_iou, |start-start|, |end-end|), so a pure time shift
240	    # leaves the score essentially unchanged — only the absolute timeline is severed. To keep the
241	    # two shifts a consistent quantity (and avoid double-rounding past _FLOAT_TOL — red-team #2),
242	    # t0_sec is ROUNDED to 6dp BEFORE subtracting (and _reset_labels rounds each endpoint first), so
243	    # the RAW reset-vs-no-reset score delta sits at the float-error floor (~3.3e-7 ≪ _FLOAT_TOL).
244	    #
245	    # HONEST LIMIT (not a bug): the trajectory shifts by an INTEGER frame count while labels shift
246	    # in SECONDS, and 6dp-rounding is NOT additive (round6(8/fps) - round6(7/fps) ≠ round6(1/fps)),
247	    # so the *serialized* 6dp boundary error (mean_start/end_error) can differ by ONE 6dp ULP
248	    # (1e-6) on a sub-second offset. That is the serialization grid, not a metric divergence — the
249	    # score is bit-identical for whole-second offsets and within _FLOAT_TOL otherwise (test #2).
